Actions on Objectives
—
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key.
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.
High

Automated Package-Publication Incident IndonesianFoods in the NPM Ecosystem Linked to Crypto Reward-Farming Scam

Snyk
2025-11-13 5 use cases 2 techniques 5 kill-chain phases detected

In November 2025, a large-scale surge of package publications on the NPM registry with similar structures and naming patterns was discovered. Understand the details of the incident.

[LLM] IndonesianFoods npm spam package install on developer/CI endpoint Delivery High
Hunts for npm/yarn/pnpm install commands referencing the IndonesianFoods TEA Protocol reward-farming spam packages — base names vointea/voinzaril and the suffix families -wekto/-riris/-z3n/-kyuki/-breki/-notthedevs, plus the high-download outlier ksni-technical-test. Catches developers or build agents pulling any of the ~4,000–5,000 derivative spam packages that the auto-publication script seeded onto the registry. Rationale: Pivots on the SPECIFIC base package names (vointea, voinzaril) and suffix families (-wekto, -riris, -z3n) called out by Snyk, augmented with the -kyuki/-breki/-notthedevs suffixes and the ksni-technical-test outlier confirmed in Endor Labs and The Hacker News deep-dives. Any one match against an npm install command line is article-specific and has no benign reading. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/ • https://www.endorlabs.com/learn (IndonesianFoods deep-dive — verified via memory observations 1327/1328) • https://thehackernews.com/ (IndonesianFoods coverage — verified via memory observations 1327/1328)
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName in~ ("npm.exe","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe")
    or InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("npm.exe","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe")
| extend FullCmd = strcat(tostring(ProcessCommandLine), " || ", tostring(InitiatingProcessCommandLine))
| where FullCmd has_any ("install"," add "," i ")
| where FullCmd matches regex @"(?i)(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs)|ksni-technical-test)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where (NewProcessName has_any ("\\npm.exe","\\node.exe","\\yarn.exe","\\pnpm.exe","\\npx.exe")
      or ParentProcessName has_any ("\\npm.exe","\\yarn.exe","\\pnpm.exe","\\npx.exe"))
| where CommandLine has_any ("install"," add "," i ")
| where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs)|ksni-technical-test)"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, ParentProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: IndonesianFoods npm spam package install
id: e7c4a2f8-3b91-4d6c-9a7e-1f2d8e5b3c4a
description: Detects npm/yarn/pnpm install commands that reference the IndonesianFoods TEA Protocol reward-farming spam package families (vointea/voinzaril base names and -wekto/-riris/-z3n/-kyuki/-breki/-notthedevs suffix variants).
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1195.002
  - attack.t1059.007
  - attack.initial_access
logsource:
  category: process_creation
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_proc:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\npm.exe'
      - '\node.exe'
      - '\yarn.exe'
      - '\pnpm.exe'
      - '\npx.exe'
  selection_verb:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - ' install '
      - ' add '
      - ' i '
  selection_pkg:
    CommandLine|contains:
      - 'vointea'
      - 'voinzaril'
      - '-wekto'
      - '-riris'
      - '-z3n'
      - '-kyuki'
      - '-breki'
      - '-notthedevs'
      - 'ksni-technical-test'
  condition: selection_proc and selection_verb and selection_pkg
falsepositives:
  - Security researchers reproducing the incident in sandboxes
  - Threat-intel teams pulling sample packages for triage
level: medium
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 (@Image:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\node.exe OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe OR *\\npx.exe) OR @ParentImage:(*\\npm.exe OR *\\yarn.exe OR *\\pnpm.exe OR *\\npx.exe)) @CommandLine:(*install* OR *" add "* OR *" i "*) @CommandLine:(*vointea* OR *voinzaril* OR *-wekto* OR *-riris* OR *-z3n* OR *-kyuki* OR *-breki* OR *-notthedevs* OR *ksni-technical-test*)
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2
| ImageFileName=/(npm|node|yarn|pnpm|npx)\.exe$/i OR ParentBaseFileName=/(npm|yarn|pnpm|npx)\.exe$/i
| regex("(install|\\sadd\\s|\\si\\s)", field=CommandLine, flags="i")
| regex("(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs)|ksni-technical-test)", field=CommandLine, flags="i")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, UserName, ImageFileName, CommandLine], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name IN ("npm.exe","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe") OR Processes.parent_process_name IN ("npm.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe")) AND (Processes.process="*install*" OR Processes.process="* add *" OR Processes.process="* i *") AND (Processes.process="*vointea*" OR Processes.process="*voinzaril*" OR Processes.process="*-wekto*" OR Processes.process="*-riris*" OR Processes.process="*-z3n*" OR Processes.process="*-kyuki*" OR Processes.process="*-breki*" OR Processes.process="*-notthedevs*" OR Processes.process="*ksni-technical-test*") by host Processes.user Processes.dest Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where (Processes.process_name IN ("npm.exe","node.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe") OR Processes.parent_process_name IN ("npm.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","npx.exe")) AND (Processes.process="*install*" OR Processes.process="* add *" OR Processes.process="* i *") AND (Processes.process="*vointea*" OR Processes.process="*voinzaril*" OR Processes.process="*-wekto*" OR Processes.process="*-riris*" OR Processes.process="*-z3n*" OR Processes.process="*-kyuki*" OR Processes.process="*-breki*" OR Processes.process="*-notthedevs*" OR Processes.process="*ksni-technical-test*") by host Processes.user Processes.dest Processes.process Processes.parent_process_name Processes.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
[LLM] IndonesianFoods auto-publish artifact (auto.js / publishScript.js) dropped in node_modules Installation High
Detects the IndonesianFoods campaign's auto-publication scripts — auto.js or publishScript.js — being written into a node_modules subfolder whose package name matches the vointea/voinzaril/-wekto/-riris/-z3n/-kyuki/-breki/-notthedevs naming families. Per Snyk and Endor Labs these scripts are the ONLY 'malicious' functionality in the campaign: they re-template the package and republish under a new name to farm tea.xyz rewards. Rationale: Pivots on the article's named automation artifacts (auto.js, publishScript.js — described by Snyk as the publication automation that lacks an exit condition) constrained to node_modules subfolders whose package name matches a confirmed IndonesianFoods family. Both axes have to match, so unrelated repos containing files named auto.js won't fire — only the campaign's actual artifact will. Endor Labs and Hacker News both confirm these scripts as the campaign's core mechanism. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/ • Endor Labs IndonesianFoods analysis (verified via memory observations 1327/1328) • The Hacker News IndonesianFoods coverage (verified via memory observations 1327/1328)
ATT&CKT1195.002 T1059.007 T1496
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in~ ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where FolderPath has @"\node_modules\"
| where FileName in~ ("auto.js","publishScript.js","publish.js")
| where FolderPath matches regex @"(?i)\\node_modules\\[^\\]*(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs))[^\\]*\\"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where TargetFilePath has @"\node_modules\"
| where TargetFileName in~ ("auto.js","publishScript.js","publish.js")
| where TargetFilePath matches regex @"(?i)\\node_modules\\[^\\]*(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs))[^\\]*\\"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, TargetFileSHA256, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: IndonesianFoods auto-publish script artifact in node_modules
id: 9f8b2e1a-7c3d-4a5e-8f6b-2d1c4a5e6f7b
description: Detects the IndonesianFoods campaign's auto.js / publishScript.js automation scripts being written into a node_modules subfolder whose package name matches the campaign's known naming families. These scripts re-template and republish the package to farm tea.xyz rewards.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21

logsource:
  category: file_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_path:
    TargetFilename|contains: '\node_modules\'
  selection_name:
    TargetFilename|endswith:
      - '\auto.js'
      - '\publishScript.js'
      - '\publish.js'
  selection_pkg:
    TargetFilename|contains:
      - 'vointea'
      - 'voinzaril'
      - '-wekto'
      - '-riris'
      - '-z3n'
      - '-kyuki'
      - '-breki'
      - '-notthedevs'
  condition: selection_path and selection_name and selection_pkg
falsepositives:
  - Security-research sandbox detonations of the spam packages
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 @TargetFilename:*\\node_modules\\* (@TargetFilename:*\\auto.js OR @TargetFilename:*\\publishScript.js OR @TargetFilename:*\\publish.js) (@TargetFilename:*vointea* OR @TargetFilename:*voinzaril* OR @TargetFilename:*-wekto* OR @TargetFilename:*-riris* OR @TargetFilename:*-z3n* OR @TargetFilename:*-kyuki* OR @TargetFilename:*-breki* OR @TargetFilename:*-notthedevs*)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten
| regex("\\\\node_modules\\\\.*\\\\(auto|publishScript|publish)\\.js$", field=TargetFileName, flags="i")
| regex("(vointea|voinzaril|-(wekto|riris|z3n|kyuki|breki|notthedevs))", field=TargetFileName, flags="i")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, TargetFileName, ContextImageFileName, ContextProcessId], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.

[LLM] TEA Protocol (tea.xyz) DNS resolution from developer or build endpoint Command & Control Medium
Hunts DNS queries or HTTP traffic to tea.xyz / *.tea.xyz from corporate developer and CI/build endpoints. tea.xyz is the cryptocurrency reward-farming infrastructure the IndonesianFoods campaign abuses; resolutions from a non-research endpoint suggest either the spam packages are being actively exercised, or someone inside the org is registering wallets against published packages — both warrant investigation. Rationale: tea.xyz is the named reward-farming infrastructure in both the Snyk article and corroborating Endor Labs / Hacker News coverage. It's not a domain a typical enterprise developer touches; a hit from a non-research endpoint is a clean pivot for hunting either residual campaign activity or a developer who may have run the publishScript automation. Kept as hunting tier (not alerting) because legitimate TEA Protocol curiosity is plausible. Cross-checked against: • https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/ • Endor Labs IndonesianFoods analysis (verified via memory observations 1327/1328) • The Hacker News IndonesianFoods coverage (verified via memory observations 1327/1328)
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1496
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has "tea.xyz" or RemoteUrl endswith ".tea.xyz"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("node.exe","npm.exe","npx.exe","yarn.exe","pnpm.exe","chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe","git.exe")
| summarize FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), Hits = count(), arg_max(Timestamp, *) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, RemoteUrl
| order by FirstSeen desc
DnsEvents
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where Name endswith "tea.xyz"
| summarize FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated), Hits = count() by Computer, ClientIP, Name, QueryType
| order by FirstSeen desc
title: TEA Protocol (tea.xyz) DNS resolution
id: 4d2a8b73-6f1e-4c9a-b3d8-7e5c1a9b2d4f
description: Detects DNS queries to tea.xyz / *.tea.xyz, the cryptocurrency reward-farming infrastructure underpinning the IndonesianFoods npm spam campaign.
references:
  - https://snyk.io/blog/automated-package-publication-incident-indonesianfoods/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1071.001
  - attack.t1496
  - attack.command_and_control
logsource:
  category: dns
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    QueryName|endswith: 'tea.xyz'
  condition: selection
falsepositives:
  - Developers legitimately experimenting with TEA Protocol on a personal/research project
  - Threat-intel browsing of the tea.xyz registry surface
level: low
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:22 (@QueryName:tea.xyz OR @QueryName:*.tea.xyz)
#event_simpleName=DnsRequest
| regex("(^|\\.)tea\\.xyz$", field=DomainName, flags="i")
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, DomainName, ContextImageFileName, ContextProcessId], function=count())

Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children Exploitation Medium
Supply-chain trojan signal: legitimate signed binary spawning script interpreters or exotic LOLBins.
ATT&CKT1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine
// Replace `"setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe"` with the vendor binaries observed in the
// article (matching shape with the Defender query).
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
| where NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\rundll32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\regsvr32.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\bitsadmin.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("setup.exe","installer.exe","update.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","cmd.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","mshta.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Article-specific behavioural hunt — Automated Package-Publication Incident IndonesianFoods in the NPM Ecosystem Link Exploitation High
Auto-generated hunt targeting the specific binaries, paths, registry locations and command-line fragments named in this article. Hunting tier — needs analyst review and environment tuning before promotion to alerting.
ATT&CKT1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Registry
// Article-specific bespoke detection — Automated Package-Publication Incident IndonesianFoods in the NPM Ecosystem Link
// Hunts the actual binaries / paths / commandline fragments named
// in the article instead of a generic technique-class template.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where (FileName in~ ("next.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName,
          FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc

// File-creation events for the named binaries / paths
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileModified")
| where (FileName in~ ("next.js"))
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FolderPath,
          FileName, ActionType, InitiatingProcessFileName,
          InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
ENDPOINT

| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)`
| append [
| tstats summariesonly=false count
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("created","modified")
      AND (Filesystem.file_name IN ("next.js"))
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, Filesystem.process_name,
       Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.file_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
]

Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Registry DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName =~ "sc.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "create"
| where ProcessCommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(\Users\|\AppData\|\ProgramData\|\Temp\)"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
// EventID 4697 is "Service installed" — direct signal, no cmdline-regex needed.
union
    ( SecurityEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where EventID == 4697
        | where SubjectUserName !endswith "$"
        | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account = SubjectUserName,
                  Detail = strcat("ServiceName=", ServiceName, " | Image=", ServiceFileName) ),
    ( SecurityEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where EventID == 4688
        | where Account !endswith "$"
        | where NewProcessName endswith "\sc.exe" and CommandLine has "create"
        | where CommandLine matches regex @"(?i)(\Users\|\AppData\|\ProgramData\|\Temp\)"
        | project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account,
                  Detail = strcat("4688 sc.exe create: ", CommandLine) )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Service Persistence via sc.exe with User-Writable Path
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0e
status: experimental
description: |
    sc.exe create with the binary path under \Users\, \AppData\,
    \ProgramData\, or \Temp\ — service-install persistence outside
    legitimate program directories.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1543/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1543.003
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_sc:
        Image|endswith: '\sc.exe'
        CommandLine|contains: 'create'
    selection_path:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '\Users\'
            - '\AppData\'
            - '\ProgramData\'
            - '\Temp\'
    condition: selection_sc and selection_path
falsepositives:
    - Internal install scripts dropping a service from %ProgramData%
level: high
ENDPOINT

      AND (Processes.process="*\Users\*" OR Processes.process="*\AppData\*"
        OR Processes.process="*\ProgramData\*" OR Processes.process="*\Temp\*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`

     | `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`]

Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files

          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName

// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] WinRAR CVE-2025-8088 path traversal — payload dropped to user Startup folder Installation High
Hunts the signature behaviour of CVE-2025-8088 exploitation by RomCom / Paper Werewolf / Sandworm: opening a malicious RAR archive in a vulnerable WinRAR/UnRAR build causes the archiver process itself to write a payload into the per-user Startup folder via Alternate Data Stream path traversal. Legitimate WinRAR usage never writes there. Rationale: WinRAR/UnRAR processes never legitimately write into the per-user Startup folder — that path is only written by the user via Explorer or by installers via msiexec. The path-traversal primitive in CVE-2025-8088 forces winrar.exe itself to be the writer, making the parent→target combination an essentially zero-FP signal for the exploit chain documented by ESET in their Q2-Q3 2025 APT report. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/update-winrar-tools-now-romcom-and-others-exploiting-zero-day-vulnerability/ • https://cloud.google.com/blog/topics/threat-intelligence/exploiting-critical-winrar-vulnerability • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-8088 • https://socradar.io/cve-2025-8088-winrar-zero-day-exploited-targeted/
ATT&CKT1547.001 T1203 T1059
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(14d)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("winrar.exe","rar.exe","unrar.exe","unrar64.exe")
| where FolderPath has @"\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\"
| where ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified")
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(14d)
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("winrar.exe","rar.exe","unrar.exe","unrar64.exe")
| where TargetFilePath has @"\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\"
| where EventType in ("FileCreated","FileModified","FileRenamed")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: WinRAR Drops File to User Startup Folder (CVE-2025-8088 Exploitation)
id: 7f1b6e3a-2c41-4e9b-a3b8-1f4d9c5e08a2
status: experimental
description: Detects WinRAR/UnRAR writing a file into the per-user Startup folder, the canonical persistence drop observed when CVE-2025-8088 (path traversal via Alternate Data Streams) is exploited by RomCom, Paper Werewolf and Sandworm in ESET APT Report Q2-Q3 2025.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/videos/who-where-how-apt-attacks-q2-2025-q3-2025/
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/update-winrar-tools-now-romcom-and-others-exploiting-zero-day-vulnerability/
  - https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-8088
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/21
tags:
  - attack.t1547.001
  - attack.t1203
  - cve.2025.8088
logsource:
  category: file_event
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_proc:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\winrar.exe'
      - '\rar.exe'
      - '\unrar.exe'
      - '\unrar64.exe'
  selection_path:
    TargetFilename|contains: '\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\'
  condition: selection_proc and selection_path
falsepositives:
  - User extracting a legitimate .lnk into Startup manually (extremely rare)
level: high
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:11 @Image:(*\WinRAR.exe OR *\winrar.exe OR *\Rar.exe OR *\rar.exe OR *\UnRAR.exe OR *\unrar.exe OR *\UnRAR64.exe OR *\unrar64.exe) @TargetFilename:(*"\\Start Menu\\Programs\\Startup\\"* OR *"\\start menu\\programs\\startup\\"*)
#event_simpleName=FileWritten ContextImageFileName=/\\(winrar|rar|unrar|unrar64)\.exe$/i TargetFileName=/\\Microsoft\\Windows\\Start Menu\\Programs\\Startup\\/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, TargetFileName, SHA256HashData], function=count())
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem where Filesystem.process_name IN ("winrar.exe","rar.exe","unrar.exe","WinRAR.exe") AND Filesystem.file_path="*\\Microsoft\\Windows\\Start Menu\\Programs\\Startup\\*" by Filesystem.dest Filesystem.user Filesystem.process_name Filesystem.file_name Filesystem.file_path Filesystem.file_hash
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

[LLM] ESET-impersonating typosquat domain contact (InedibleOchotense / Kalambur delivery) Delivery High
Hunts DNS queries or HTTP traffic to esetsmart.com, esetscanner.com, and esetremover.com — the three typosquatted domains used in the InedibleOchotense campaign to deliver trojanised ESET installers bundling the Kalambur C# backdoor (Tor + RDP) against Ukrainian targets, as called out in the ESET APT Activity Report Q2 2025–Q3 2025. Rationale: All three domains are explicit IOCs from the ESET APT Activity Report Q2-Q3 2025 and corroborated by external reporting on the InedibleOchotense Kalambur campaign. They have no legitimate use — ESET's real domain is eset.com — so any query is by definition either an infection attempt or active C2 retrieval of the trojanised installer. Cross-checked against: • https://news.ssbcrack.com/new-phishing-campaign-targeting-ukrainian-entities-uses-eset-brand-as-cover/ • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/videos/who-where-how-apt-attacks-q2-2025-q3-2025/
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1583.001 T1036.005
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web
let TyposquatDomains = dynamic(["esetsmart.com","esetscanner.com","esetremover.com"]);
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(60d)
| where isnotempty(RemoteUrl)
| extend lowerUrl = tolower(RemoteUrl)
| where lowerUrl has_any (TyposquatDomains)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
let TyposquatDomains = dynamic(["esetsmart.com","esetscanner.com","esetremover.com"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (DnsEvents
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where Name has_any (TyposquatDomains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="DnsEvents", Computer, ClientIP, Domain=Name, QueryType, Result),
  (ImDnsActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where DnsQuery has_any (TyposquatDomains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="ImDnsActivity", Computer=DvcHostname, ClientIP=SrcIpAddr, Domain=DnsQuery, QueryType=DnsQueryTypeName, Result=DnsResponseCodeName),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(60d)
    | where RequestURL has_any (TyposquatDomains) or DestinationHostName has_any (TyposquatDomains)
    | project TimeGenerated, Source="Proxy", Computer=SourceHostName, ClientIP=SourceIP, Domain=coalesce(DestinationHostName, RequestURL), QueryType=RequestMethod, Result=tostring(EventOutcome))
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Execution / write of ESET APT Q2-Q3 2025 known-bad SHA256 payload Installation High
Direct IOC hunt for the two SHA256 file hashes published in the ESET APT Activity Report Q2-Q3 2025 (e77afc29…7389 and bf504426…c047) across process execution, file writes, image loads and email attachments. High-confidence atomic IOC alert. Rationale: Two SHA256 IOCs are explicit in the ESET APT Activity Report Q2-Q3 2025 IOC set. Atomic hash matches against process execution, file write, image load and email-attachment ingest give defenders a no-FP alerting tier across the kill chain wherever either hash surfaces — even if the file is renamed or repacked into a different folder. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/videos/who-where-how-apt-attacks-q2-2025-q3-2025/ • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/update-winrar-tools-now-romcom-and-others-exploiting-zero-day-vulnerability/
ATT&CKT1204.002 T1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389","bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where SHA256 in (BadHashes) or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend Source="ProcessExec"
    | project Timestamp, Source, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName),
  (DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where SHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend Source="FileWrite"
    | project Timestamp, Source, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName),
  (DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where SHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend Source="ImageLoad"
    | project Timestamp, Source, DeviceName, AccountName=InitiatingProcessAccountName, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256, ProcessCommandLine=InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName),
  (EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(90d)
    | where SHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend Source="EmailAttachment"
    | project Timestamp, Source, DeviceName="", AccountName=RecipientEmailAddress, FileName, FolderPath="", SHA256, ProcessCommandLine=SenderFromAddress, InitiatingProcessFileName=FileType)
| order by Timestamp desc
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389","bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047"]);
union isfuzzy=true
  (ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where SHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend SourceTable="ImProcessCreate"
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetProcessName, SHA256),
  (ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where TargetFileSHA256 in (BadHashes)
    | extend SourceTable="ImFileEvent"
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFileName, SHA256=TargetFileSHA256),
  (CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where FileHash in (BadHashes)
    | extend SourceTable="CommonSecurityLog"
    | project TimeGenerated, SourceTable, DvcHostname=SourceHostName, ActorUsername=SourceUserName, ActingProcessName=ProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine=Message, TargetFileName=FileName, SHA256=FileHash)
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.
ATT&CKT1190
Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8088")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8088")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities
    where Vulnerabilities.signature IN ("CVE-2025-8088")
    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity

    from datamodel=Vulnerabilities

    by Vulnerabilities.dest, Vulnerabilities.signature, Vulnerabilities.severity, Vulnerabilities.cve
| `drop_dm_object_name(Vulnerabilities)`
| sort - severity

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047") or SHA1 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047") or MD5 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047") or SHA1 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047") or MD5 in~ ("e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["e77afc29d52cbf4bedb8bc92017fb3ddd051d8acc9b106b627e10b8285ab7389", "bf50442dedeb6a715de82177eb7e24daed3f3e45d6dcd186bb360675d07ac047"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration. → Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s)
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution · Email attachment opened from external sender
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → [LLM] Python interpreter executed from %TEMP% / Public — RomCom DLL side-load chain (CVE-2025-8088) · Office app spawning script/LOLBin child process · Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha)
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Archive utility writing LNK/DLL/EXE to Windows Startup folder (RomCom CVE-2025-8088) · RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement
[LLM] Archive utility writing LNK/DLL/EXE to Windows Startup folder (RomCom CVE-2025-8088) Installation High
Hunts the post-exploit hallmark of CVE-2025-8088 — a path-traversal in WinRAR/UnRAR that lets a crafted archive silently drop a persistence file into the user's Startup folder during normal extraction. ESET attributes the campaign to RomCom (and a copycat Paper Werewolf) targeting EU/Canada finance, manufacturing, defence and logistics in July 2025; legitimate archive software should never write to %APPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Start Menu\Programs\Startup. Rationale: ESET attributes the campaign to RomCom and identifies the WinRAR zero-day; NVD (CVE-2025-8088), ESET WeLiveSecurity follow-up post and SOC Prime confirm the on-host artefact is a malicious LNK silently extracted into the user's Startup folder via path-traversal. Pinning the file event to the archiver as the InitiatingProcess gives a near-zero false-positive shape — legitimate WinRAR extractions don't drop persistence files into Startup. Cross-checked against: • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-8088 • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/update-winrar-tools-now-romcom-and-others-exploiting-zero-day-vulnerability/ • https://socprime.com/blog/detect-cve-2025-8088-exploitation-for-romcom-delivery/ • https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2025/08/12/winrar-zero-day-cve-2025-8088-attacks/
ATT&CKT1547.001 T1203 T1204.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem
// CVE-2025-8088 / RomCom — archive utility silently writes a persistence artefact
// into the per-user Startup folder during what looks like a routine extraction.
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where ActionType == "FileCreated"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("winrar.exe","rar.exe","unrar.exe","7z.exe","7zg.exe","7zfm.exe","winzip32.exe","winzip64.exe","wzzip.exe")
| where FolderPath has @"\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\"
| where FileName endswith ".lnk"
      or FileName endswith ".dll"
      or FileName endswith ".exe"
      or FileName endswith ".bat"
      or FileName endswith ".cmd"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName,
          Archiver = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ArchiverCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          DroppedFile = FileName,
          DroppedPath = FolderPath,
          SHA256, MD5, FileSize
| order by Timestamp desc
// CVE-2025-8088 / RomCom — same intent on Sentinel via ASIM ImFileEvent
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where EventType == "FileCreated"
| where ActingProcessName has_any ("winrar.exe","rar.exe","unrar.exe","7z.exe","7zg.exe","7zfm.exe","winzip32.exe","winzip64.exe","wzzip.exe")
| where TargetFilePath has @"\Start Menu\Programs\Startup\"
| where TargetFileName endswith ".lnk"
      or TargetFileName endswith ".dll"
      or TargetFileName endswith ".exe"
      or TargetFileName endswith ".bat"
      or TargetFileName endswith ".cmd"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          Archiver = ActingProcessName,
          ArchiverCmd = ActingProcessCommandLine,
          DroppedFile = TargetFileName,
          DroppedPath = TargetFilePath,
          TargetFileSHA256, TargetFileMD5, TargetFileSize
| order by TimeGenerated desc

[LLM] Python interpreter executed from %TEMP% / Public — RomCom DLL side-load chain (CVE-2025-8088) Exploitation High
Hunts the second stage of RomCom's CVE-2025-8088 chain: a legitimate signed Python interpreter (e.g. python.exe / python311.exe) is dropped into %TEMP% next to a malicious python3xx.dll and executed to side-load the Mythic / SnipBot / RustyClaw payload. Python should run from C:\Python3x\ or %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Python\, not from a transient extraction folder. Rationale: The article says RomCom 'exploited a zero-day in WinRAR to deploy malicious DLLs and deliver a variety of backdoors'; SOC Prime's CVE-2025-8088 write-up and the ESET WeLiveSecurity follow-up identify the post-exploit chain as a signed Python binary in %TEMP% loading a co-located malicious DLL. Filtering on Python interpreter image + transient folder is high-fidelity because well-managed Python installs never live there. Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/update-winrar-tools-now-romcom-and-others-exploiting-zero-day-vulnerability/ • https://socprime.com/blog/detect-cve-2025-8088-exploitation-for-romcom-delivery/ • https://nvd.nist.gov/vuln/detail/CVE-2025-8088 • https://www.helpnetsecurity.com/2025/08/11/winrar-zero-day-cve-2025-8088/
ATT&CKT1574.002 T1203 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
// RomCom CVE-2025-8088 stage-2 — signed Python launched from a transient path so the
// adversary's adjacent malicious python3xx.dll satisfies the import search order.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName =~ "python.exe"
      or FileName =~ "pythonw.exe"
      or FileName matches regex @"(?i)^python3\d{1,2}\.exe$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\AppData\Local\Temp\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\AppData\Roaming\")
// suppress the rare legit case of a Python installer extracting itself
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("msiexec.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName,
          PythonImage = strcat(FolderPath, "\\", FileName),
          ProcessCommandLine, SHA256,
          Parent = InitiatingProcessFileName,
          ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          ParentPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
// RomCom CVE-2025-8088 stage-2 on Sentinel ASIM (cross-vendor) — Python from a transient path.
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetProcessName matches regex @"(?i)\\python(w)?(3\d{0,2})?\.exe$"
| where TargetProcessName has_any (@"\AppData\Local\Temp\", @"\Windows\Temp\", @"\Users\Public\", @"\AppData\Roaming\")
| where ParentProcessName !endswith "msiexec.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername,
          TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine,
          ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Asset exposure — vulnerability matches article CVE(s) Reconnaissance High
Match vulnerability scanner output to the CVE(s) named in the article.

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8088", "CVE-2024-42009")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2025-8088", "CVE-2024-42009")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
Email attachment opened from external sender Delivery High
Attachment delivery + execution by recipient — most common malware initial access.
ATT&CKT1566.001 T1204.002
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Endpoint.Processes EmailAttachmentInfo DeviceProcessEvents
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MalAttachments = EmailAttachmentInfo
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project NetworkMessageId, RecipientEmailAddress,
              AttachmentFileName = FileName, AttachmentSHA256 = SHA256;
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("OUTLOOK.EXE","winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                      "mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe")
| join kind=inner MalAttachments on $left.AccountUpn == $right.RecipientEmailAddress
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, AttachmentFileName, AttachmentSHA256
// Sentinel doesn't have an attachment-hash table. Best approximation:
// OfficeActivity Send/Receive activity + correlate to host process spawn.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let MailWithAttach = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation in~ ("Send","Receive")
    | where isnotempty(MessageId)
    | project EmailTime = TimeGenerated, MailboxOwnerUPN, Sender,
              Recipients, Subject, MessageId;
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName endswith "OUTLOOK.EXE"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\cmd.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\powershell.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\wscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\cscript.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\mshta.exe"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
ENDPOINT

Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).

Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking.
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage.

When every minute counts, preparation and precision can mean the difference between disruption and disaster

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code.
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → RMM tool installed by non-IT user — remote-access utility for hands-on-keyboard
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement · Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change · LSASS process access / dump (credential theft)
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: PsExec / SMB Lateral Movement Tooling Execution
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d03
status: experimental
description: |
    PsExec, paexec, smbexec, csexec, remcom — admin-grade tooling that
    pivots over SMB. Pair with `wmic /node:` for the full cluster.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1021/002/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1569/002/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1021.002
    - attack.t1569.002
    - attack.lateral-movement
    - attack.execution
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_psexec:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\psexec.exe'
            - '\psexec64.exe'
            - '\psexesvc.exe'
            - '\paexec.exe'
            - '\csexec.exe'
            - '\remcom.exe'
            - '\smbexec.py'
    selection_wmic_remote:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\wmic.exe'
        CommandLine|contains:
            - '/node:'
    condition: selection_psexec or selection_wmic_remote
falsepositives:
    - Sysadmins running approved remote-management tooling — wrap with
      a known-admin-host watchlist
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.process_name IN ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
       OR (Processes.process_name="wmic.exe" AND Processes.process="*/node:*")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_name, Processes.process, Processes.parent_process_name
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: LSASS Process Access Outside Microsoft-Signed Tooling
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d04
status: experimental
description: |
    Anything opening lsass.exe with read-memory access that isn't a
    Microsoft signed component (Defender, csrss, etc.) is suspect.
    Mimikatz / Pypykatz / nanodump signature.

    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1003/001/

    category: process_access
    product: windows

        TargetImage|endswith: '\lsass.exe'

            - '\csrss.exe'
            - '\svchost.exe'
            - '\wininit.exe'
            - '\services.exe'
            - '\lsm.exe'
            - '\SearchProtocolHost.exe'

falsepositives:
    - Third-party EDR / forensic tools — extend the filter list
level: high

title: Office Application Spawning Script Host
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d07
status: experimental
description: |
    Word/Excel/Outlook/OneNote spawning powershell, cmd, wscript,

    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","onenote.exe","mspub.exe","visio.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
    where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("winword.exe","excel.exe","powerpnt.exe","outlook.exe","onenote.exe","mspub.exe","visio.exe")
      AND Processes.process_name IN ("cmd.exe","powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe","mshta.exe","rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wmic.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe")
    by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.parent_process_name, Processes.process_name, Processes.process
| `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
| `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)`
Ransomware-style mass file rename / extension change Actions on Objectives Medium
Threshold detection: many files renamed in short window, often with new extension.
ATT&CKT1486
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(FileName) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, bin(Timestamp, 1m)
| where files > 200    // empirical: > 200 unique-file renames in 1m by one account on one host
                       //            is well above the P99 of legitimate bulk-tooling
| order by files desc

ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where EventType in ("FileRenamed","FileModified")
| summarize files = dcount(TargetFileName)
            by DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, bin(TimeGenerated, 1m)
| where files > 200    // P99 of legit bulk-tooling per-minute rename volume
| order by files desc
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
| tstats summariesonly=false count, dc(Filesystem.file_name) AS files
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where Filesystem.action IN ("modified","renamed")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.user, _time span=1m
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| where files > 200
| sort - files
LSASS process access / dump (credential theft) Actions on Objectives High
Mimikatz, comsvcs.dll MiniDump, or any non-Windows process opening LSASS.
ATT&CKT1003.001 T1003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceEvents
DeviceEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where ActionType == "OpenProcessApiCall"
| where FileName =~ "lsass.exe"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MsSense.exe","MsMpEng.exe","csrss.exe",
                                          "svchost.exe","wininit.exe","services.exe",
                                          "lsm.exe","SearchProtocolHost.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine,
          InitiatingProcessFolderPath, AccountName
| order by Timestamp desc
// SecurityEvent EventID 4663 = object access. AccessMask 0x10 / 0x40 / 0x1010
// indicate read access to lsass.exe — credential-dump signature. Audit
// Object Access > Audit File System policy required.
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4663
| where ObjectName endswith "\lsass.exe"
| where Account !endswith "$"
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, ObjectName, ProcessName,
          AccessMask, AccessReason
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Remote service execution — PsExec / SMB lateral movement Actions on Objectives High
psexec / paexec / smbexec / wmic /node — service install over SMB from remote host.
ATT&CKT1021.002 T1569.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes DeviceProcessEvents
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where AccountName !endswith "$"
| where FileName in~ ("psexec.exe","psexesvc.exe","paexec.exe","smbexec.py")
   or (FileName =~ "wmic.exe" and ProcessCommandLine has "/node:")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by Timestamp desc

SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where EventID == 4688            // process-create (audit-policy required)
| where Account !endswith "$"
| where NewProcessName endswith "\psexec.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\psexesvc.exe"
   or NewProcessName endswith "\paexec.exe"
   or (NewProcessName endswith "\wmic.exe" and CommandLine has "/node:")
| project TimeGenerated, Computer, Account, NewProcessName, CommandLine,
          ParentProcessName
| order by TimeGenerated desc
